ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e2905	State press support was introduced in 1969, in the same year I was born – without drawing any further comparisons. The main objective of the aid was to maintain a differentiated daily press and maintain the local newspaper price. The aid scheme has been subject to some changes since then. Forty-five years is something – also for an aid scheme. And it's been a raging development, not least on the digital side, at this time. Several changes have been made, supported by a broad political majority. The production grant has been converted, from a subsidy reserved for paper newspapers to a platform neutral grant. A dynamic subsidy cap has been introduced. The total grant per year may reach a maximum of 40 pst. the operating costs of the individual media company – to ensure that the individual beneficiary does not become too dependent on the grant. During its period of consultation, the Stoltenberg Government sent out a proposal to establish an amount ceiling for the amount of annual grants received by a single media company. It was suggested that the roof should be set at 40 million. kr. This is proposed followed up in this case. It is therefore a little surprising that the Workers' Party is now, shortly after, against this roof. The aim of introducing a static aid ceiling is to ensure that support provides a greater basis for continued diversity of publications in the Norwegian media landscape. In the past, there has been a broad political consensus that the production grant should be directed more towards the smallest localities, on the grounds that these were particularly important for local democracy. Nevertheless, in recent years the large newspapers have appeared to have received more support, at the expense of the small ones. In my hometown, Nittedal, it is the locality Varingen that applies. If you haven't read it, you're not into what's going on. Around the country, there are many of these who share in keeping local democracy alive. A static roof could have released funds and targeted the system, so that several of the small newspapers had more to go with. Instead, the majority in this Chamber chooses to vote in favour of a mechanism which, irrespective of the size of the aid, gives more to those who already have the most.

This has been the case since the law of motor travel in the fields and watercourses was adopted in 1977 because then Minister of Environmental Protection Gro Harlem Brundtland saw that pleasure driving with scooters had gone over the stick and stone. Two thirds of the population — a solid majority of the population — will not have unnecessary snowmobile rides in nature, showing a mapping by the Environment Directorate. ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e3119	On 15 November 2013, Minister for Climate and Environment Tine Sundtoft sent out a press release that the government is implementing and extending the pilot scheme that allows municipalities to establish snowmobile loops for pleasure driving. The extended testing scheme shall apply for the period 2014-2018. The scheme, initially limited to 40 municipalities, is extended so that all municipalities that have notified their interest will participate, a total of 108 municipalities. In parallel with the implementation and extension of the experiment, the Ministry has started work on a letter of consultation on changes to the law. The government intends to implement a legislative change during the current majority period before the trial is completed and final evaluation report is available. The Christian People's Party is critical of such a procedure, because it disregards a central part of the law on motor travel in the fields and watercourses, without a legislative amendment being presented and adopted by the Stortinget. Central interests have not been heard. The government thus achieves that a desired legislative change can take effect with immediate effect without the prior legislative change process. Significant uncertainty has been created as to whether the extension of the test scheme is in line with the intention of the Test Act. Several independent lawyers and Stortingen's investigation department have shown that the trial is not in line with the law. The government’s argument about the legality of the extended trial scheme is not convincing. I will refer to the Civil Ombudsman's statement on 12 June, where he writes the following: ‘I have come to the conclusion that the scope of the pilot scheme is outside the scope of the Test Act. The Ministry should therefore review the pilot scheme and, in particular, the need for the number of municipalities.» I believe that the government now has to place the decisive emphasis on the assessment that the Civil Ombudsman comes with. If the government does not, the Storting must ensure that the Test Act is practised within the framework of the Test Act. The Christian People's Party believes that the government should set aside the experimental system and concentrate on developing and presenting as soon as possible a proposal to amend the law on motor travel in land and watercourses for treatment in Stortinget. The Christian People's Party concluded in the previous ageing period that all Norwegian municipalities should have the same opportunity as the eight test municipalities have had since 2000 to increase local self-government over motor travel in the outland. Motor travel shall only take place within defined areas and within strict national guidelines. Increased municipal autonomy presupposes that municipalities assume their responsibility for safeguarding important natural and outdoor interests. Over the period 2000–2005, a pilot project with expanded local autonomy was carried out in eight selected municipalities. The municipalities that have been involved in the project have been allowed to establish both tourist loops and recreational loops. The experiment has been ex-post evaluated by the Norwegian Institute for Natural Research on behalf of the Ministry of Environment. The evaluation shows that all six intermediate targets for better motor traffic were fully or partially met in the experimental municipalities. In fact, the population of municipalities without snowmobile loops was more plagued by noise than the population of municipalities participating in the testing scheme. I would like to remind you that an important overall objective of the test scheme was that the purpose clause of the Motor Travel Act should be better achieved than today. The objective paragraph is based on three main elements: overall societal vision, protection of the natural environment and the promotion of well-being. The Planning and Building Act allowed the creation of their own loops for recreational purposes, which was done in some municipalities, in others not. I believe that the evaluation just shows that municipalities are able to make these balances in a good way, and that it is fully possible to maintain a restrictive national motor vehicle policy – even if an open-ended local self-government and open to local variations. The Christian People's Party therefore wants to open up to increased local self-government – with the same purpose and limitations as existed before the experimental scheme.

Many of this work will have positive consequences for individuals and small businesses. In addition, the Scheel Committee will continue its work on a growth-promoting tax system. However, the Committee believes that there is a need for a review of the framework conditions for individuals, precisely because of their specificity and their potential, so that they can contribute more to future growth and value creation. The form of company is the most common form of company in Norway. The rules of the Share Act are therefore of great importance to traders in the country. The main weight of the limited liability companies is small and medium-sized enterprises. One must therefore look at these companies' needs and ensure that the rules of the Share Act are well adapted to these companies. Even if the Shares Act is to maintain and balance more and partly conflicting interests, it should ensure that the rules do not impose unnecessary burdens on the companies. In the government platform, we can read that the government will develop and simplify legislation for small and medium-sized enterprises. In addition to the representative proposal from the Left, the Right has proposed that the Government consider whether a regulation establishing small enterprises as compensation for personal companies as a legal entity should be introduced. An example of such an organisation may be a micro-AS. It should be a company organisation which has the advantages of a limited liability company but which at the same time has framework conditions and requirements which make it as simple as possible to operate such a company. The idea of creating a separate stock law for small companies or micro-liability companies has been discussed before. The Board of Auditors proposed in 2009 to introduce the micro-liability company which owns a form of company with a correspondingly simpler share and accounting law. In the EU, the Microfinance Directive was adopted in 2012. It gave companies with a turnover of less than 5.6 million. kr the ability to operate an account in accordance with simplified rules with very few note information. These companies also do not need to produce an annual report. This Directive is not yet incorporated into the EEA Agreement, but there is good reason to believe that it will. There has been a change some years ago whereby Member States are allowed to exempt micro-enterprises from the obligation to prepare annual accounts. It is for each EEA Member State to decide whether the exemption for so-called micro-enterprises is desirable for national reasons. The Directive intends to facilitate the reduction of the administrative burden on micro-enterprises. It therefore provides for simplified framework conditions for micro-enterprises. However, in order to ensure adequate protection for any investor, the micro-enterprises under the Directive should continue to be subject to obligations under national law in order to keep an overview of business transactions and the financial position of the companies. This Directive is very EEA-relevant and acceptable. The entities subject to reporting are set out in the Accounting Act. The Accounting Act does not provide that micro-enterprises as defined in the Directive may be exempted from the obligation to draw up annual accounts. ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e3803	This proposal raises a necessary and topical debate. At the same time, the food industry itself has initiated the use of sustainable palm oil in food through the initiative for the use of sustainable palm oil in Norway, where the companies behind this initiative undertake to use only sustainably produced palm oil or certified palm oil according to the criteria of the Round Table for Sustainable Palm Oil, RSPO, by the end of 2015. This is currently the world’s most well-known palm oil certification scheme, and the Rain Forest Fund states that it is significantly better to buy RSPO certified palm oil than uncertified palm oil. Each of the companies behind this initiative undertakes to explain the amount of palm oil they use and the proportion certified according to the criteria of the RSPO or through other certification and control systems. Holdings shall also report the traceable proportion of palm oil. This is an affordable initiative by the industry itself and will be an important element in ensuring that palm oil used and traded in Norway is sustainable. In the Christian People's Party, we are concerned that it should be sustainable, traceable palm oil, and that the information to consumers on the use of palm oil in food should be good. ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e3851	It's an important issue that the big-time representatives Raja and Kjenseth are focusing on. In the debate on the use of palm oil, there are actually two aspects we are discussing: the importance for health and the environmental impact. When the palm oil is often referred to as ‘hosting’, it is because it is an unfavourable variant among the oils. Palm oil contains significantly more saturated fat than other vegetable oils. The health authorities therefore recommend that we limit the intake of saturated fatty acids. In order to ensure a good diet, it is important that we can make informed choices when dealing with food. When the EU regulation regulating the information and labelling of food content enters into force in Norway at the turn of the year, we will have new provisions that will make it easier for consumers to know what the food contains. The list of ingredients for prepacked foods will be required to show the type of vegetable oil or fat contained in the product, such as palm oil or rapeseed oil. The purpose of the new labelling provisions is primarily to help consumers make healthy food choices. It will also have the added effect that consumers who wish to do so can choose from an environmental point of view away food containing palm oil. This leads me to the second important challenge, namely the environmental aspect. The oil palm produces relatively much oil per hectare and is therefore a more land efficient product than most alternative food oils. The production of palm oil remains a significant cause of deforestation in tropical countries, particularly in Southeast Asia. Of course, it's very unfortunate. But palm oil is also an important source of income for many poor countries, and can be produced sustainably. It is not palm oil as a product that creates the negative environmental effects, but how land is used as an input to production. Systems are currently in place to ensure that traceable palm oil can be purchased. Several have been involved in the RSPO system, which is one of the world’s most famous certification schemes for palm oil. When palm oil is sold as Identity Preserved, it means that certified palm oil is kept separate from non-certified palm oil throughout the supply chain. In addition, it is traceable, so that the buyer can know which plantations the palm oil comes from. Unfortunately, small IP palm oil is currently available on the market. In order to distort production from unsustainable to sustainable palm oil, it is important that businesses and consumers demand sustainable and traceable palm oil and that we, as authorities, focus on this. Fortunately, we see a trend in the positive direction. Over 50 pst. the international palm oil market is now subject to strict internal sustainability criteria. But it is important that this development continues. The government is also working to combat unsustainable palm oil through climate and forest management. One of the measures is to support countries that reduce their deforestation. The value of intact forests is often not taken into account in the land use decisions. By rewarding land for intact forests, we contribute to the environmental importance of forests in land use decisions. The government also supports measures to facilitate conversion to sustainable and deforestation-free production, including: through a public-private partnership with the Consumer Goods Forum, which is a professional organisation with more than 400 members and a total sale of more than 20 trillion – or 1000 billion. - Kroner. The partnership is at the heart of the deforestation problem — expanding and inefficient agricultural production. The government will also continue to focus its search for non-sustainable production of palm oil. We believe that today's instruments with existing rules on labelling and traceability, as well as the own involvement of authorities, consumers and businesses in the matter, are sufficient to ensure proper development in this matter. But, of course, we need to follow developments closely so that other measures can be assessed if developments do not go as we please.

ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e3922	In view of the fact that this is a free trade agreement signed in 2008, I am pleased and relieved to see that it can finally be ratified and will soon enter into force. A unanimous nutrition committee in Stortinget has now said and given its green light. This free trade agreement will provide Norway with much better market access in Colombia for industrial products and fish. This will include, inter alia: say full elimination of customs duties for 85 pst. from the date of entry into force of the Agreement, exports of industrial products by EFTA. Customs duties for the remaining goods shall be removed over four and nine years respectively. Norwegian enterprises also have better opportunities to participate in tendering for public acquisitions. Today, Colombia has customs duties on imports of fish and seafood in the sheath 5-20 pst. The agreement provides for a total of 28 fishery products to be freed from customs duties from day one, while a gradual phasing down of 130 other fishery products. Known fishery products such as fresh salmon and trout, fresh halibut, fresh cod, frozen salmon, frozen herring and frozen mackerel are included in the 28 fishery products which get zero-tariff when the agreement enters into force. In other words, this agreement provides very good conditions for the export of fish and marine products. I believe that this is something the Norwegian fishing industry will enjoy very well in the years to come. In addition, Norwegian exports of services are better conditioned. The Economic and Social Committee had a hearing on this matter, and stressed, inter alia, that: Norwegian Industry that this was a very important agreement for their members. If I'm going to bring forward one company that should lift the flags and celebrate the ratification of the agreement, that's enough Yara. Fertilizers are Norway’s most important export product to Colombia. Fertilizers are currently subject to a customs duty of 10 p.m. on importation into Colombia. The customs duties for this product will be removed immediately after the entry into force of the Free Trade Agreement, so that the free trade agreement will be beneficial to Norwegian fertilizer exports in competition with foreign producers. Of other typical Norwegian exports where customs duties are eliminated immediately, aluminium, ship’s pumps and solar cells are. For crude oil, propane, parts of drilling machines and newsprint rolls there will be a gradual slowdown of customs duties. Today, imports from Colombia to Norway are mainly agricultural products such as coffee, flowers and fruit. In 2013, we imported goods for approx. 787 million. kr. I would like to think that this will take a heavy toll, especially for fruit and vegetables – among other things. otherwise I know that there are popular fruits in Colombia that are not bought in Norway today. In this area, Norway will provide Colombia with at least as good conditions as given to the EU. Norwegian exports to Colombia last year amounted to 222 million. kr. When the Storting agrees to the ratification of this Free Trade Agreement, which has already been ratified by the other EFTA countries, a Norwegian export adventure is facilitated. The possibilities are there, and one can of course dream of experiencing the same success as one did when the free trade agreement with South Korea entered into force – a country that has about as many inhabitants as Colombia and where Norway exports about. Between 13 and 14 B.C.E. kr annually. However, one thing is to facilitate better opportunities for Norwegian exports – that there are equal or better conditions in relation to other countries. Another issue is whether this agreement had not been ratified. At that time, we, the Stortinget, had allowed Norwegian companies and companies to become in a competitive situation – for the benefit of Colombia’s other trading partners in the United States, the EU and other EFTA countries – and get worse conditions, which Norwegian companies have suffered for several years. There is no concealment that we are concerned about the human rights situation and workers' rights in Colombia. We are therefore pleased that Norway has a significant humanitarian commitment in Colombia, both through assistance to Norwegian, international and local organisations, and which facilitates the peace negotiations between the Colombian Government and the FARC and ELN guild groups. Norway and Colombia have also signed a bilateral Memorandum of Understanding, MoU, on political consultations with reference to workers’ rights and human rights. I would also like to stress that the Committee has noted that, by letter to the Committee dated 13 May, the Minister for Foreign Affairs confirms that the Government will actively follow up on issues related to the rights of workers and human rights in future consultations with Colombia. ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e4035	I very much appreciate that the Business Committee unanimously agreed to the ratification of a free trade agreement between EFTA and Colombia and an agricultural agreement between Norway and Colombia. The main objective of the agreement is to stimulate increased trade and economic cooperation between the EFTA countries and Colombia. Trade between Norway and Colombia is modest today, but increasing. We expect a further increase as a result of this agreement. In addition, the agreement will ensure that the competitiveness of Norwegian companies is not impaired compared to major international competitors, including companies established in the EU. Let me start by giving some examples of what the Free Trade Agreement will mean for Norwegian exporters. The free trade agreement significantly improves market access for industrial products and fish. There will be full elimination of customs duties for most Norwegian exports from the entry into force of the Agreement. Customs duties on the remaining goods shall be removed after a maximum of nine years. Fertilizers are Norway’s most important export product to Colombia. Fertilizers are currently subject to a customs duty of 10 p.m. into Colombia. The customs duties applicable to this product shall be removed immediately upon entry into force of the free trade agreement. It'll mean a lot to Yara's factory in Her Island. Not only does it ensure jobs, but it also facilitates increased activity. The rates of duty in Colombia on fish are currently between 5 and 20 pst. Norwegian exports of these products to Colombia are currently limited. The Free Trade Agreement facilitates an increase in exports by eliminating customs duties either immediately or after a period of deceleration. Colombia has made little use of the WTO shipping obligations. In the Free Trade Agreement, Colombia has removed all restrictions on international shipping as well as for certain types of domestic maritime transport. This is an area where Norwegian shipping companies have strong export interests. Norway has strong export interests in maritime insurance, and Colombia is committed to giving Norwegian operators access to the Colombian market under the same conditions as national operators. Colombia also has strong oil and gas deposits, and production has increased significantly in recent years. Norwegian operators are given market access in the agreement for a wide range of energy-related services. A number of Norwegian importers have contacted the Ministry of Industry and Fisheries with questions about when the agreement will enter into force. Norway is reducing the rates of customs duties on flowers in the Free Trade Agreement, and we expect, in particular, that imports of roses will increase. Norway eliminates the rate of customs duty on textiles and the rate of duty on a number of agricultural products. The free trade agreement with Colombia is controversial in Norway. This is due to the difficult situation in the country with regard to labour rights and human rights. The agreement has also been criticised for not containing more obligatory provisions on employee rights than those contained in the legal provisions. The government has taken care of this. We have signed a MoU with Colombia on political consultations. The MoU contains explicit references to employee rights and human rights. The first consultation was held in March. The Colombian delegation was led by Minister of Labour Pardo, and several other relevant actors in the field of human rights were involved. I can assure you that employees' rights will be given high priority in the further follow-up of the MoU. The aim is to hold the next political consultations in Colombia in a few months. In addition to employee rights, other human rights issues will also be addressed. Norway is a significant partner and contributor to the work on human rights in Colombia. The government is determined to continue and strengthen our commitment to the peace process. I can also confirm that we will of course keep Stortinget informed about developments. In addition to following up on the MoU, we will work to extend the Free Trade Agreement with a chapter on trade and sustainable development. Such a chapter will contain binding provisions on employee rights and the environment. The first occasion will be at the first meeting of the Joint Committee. This was something I took up with Minister of Labour Pardo during a meeting we had in March. It is not the case that the FTA will solve Colombia’s challenges. Nor is it the intention to conclude such agreements. We conclude free trade agreements in order to maximise the contribution to increased value creation and well-being in Norway. At the same time, the agreement opens up dialogue with Colombia and the growing presence of Norwegian companies can have a positive impact on the working conditions of the country. We want to influence by presence and dialogue, and believe that this free trade agreement will be an important contribution to this.

ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e4149	Several have not asked for the floor on case No. 17.
ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e4163	Norway is part of Europe and European cooperation. The financial crisis showed us that cross-border cooperation is important for solving international problems. Work is a central value for all people. The experience of being out of the labour market is serious, and many young people in Europe experience unemployment. We have a common commitment to get young people into permanent work. The alternative is young people who lose faith in the politician’s ability to solve their challenges. The EU Programme EaSI aims to contribute to the implementation of the Europe 2020 strategy by providing assistance to meet the EU’s objectives of promoting a high level of employment, ensuring decent social protection, combating social exclusion and poverty and improving working conditions. EURES is a cooperation network between the European Commission and the PES in the EU Member States, the EEA States and Switzerland. Cooperation shall promote the free movement of labour and strengthen employment opportunities for all in the EEA and Switzerland. Cooperation assists in the provision of recruitment and placement services for employers and jobseekers, supported by information and guidance. It is a challenge of high unemployment among young people because they may lose faith in finding a job and give up applying for job vacancies in other European countries with lower unemployment. The EURES programme is intended to prevent this. It provides for the modernisation of the EURES portal and its self-service tools. Among other things, through the further development of a mobility scheme called your first EURES job. The portal will prepare young people's access to the labour market throughout Europe. The target group is young people aged 18 to 30 who want to find a job in another Member State, and employers from small and medium-sized enterprises who can offer jobs to young jobseekers. The EURES portal has some 38 million individual visits per year. The European Commission states that around 150 000 jobseekers find work each year or receive job offers through EURES cooperation. The experience of cooperation within the EURES network has been positive and is an instrument of Norwegian labour market policy related to mobility and workers and labour migration. The government wants Norway to be a contributor to the creation of a Europe of solidarity, inclusive and secure. Job seekers will be able to obtain accurate information on working conditions, salary levels and more general information on the conditions of the countries covered by the cooperation and where they can expect to apply for work. In Norway, participation in EURES is managed by the Employment and Welfare Act. Norway has participated in EURES since 1994. Due to budgetary constraints, it is proposed that Norway participates from 2014 only in the EURES axis of the EaSI programme, which is the reason for – and not the other parts of the Regulation. Full participation in the EaSI programme will commit budgetary resources over several years and will require a separate allocation proposal for the Storting. The Working Party has proposed to the Committee that the Storting give its consent to a decision on the inclusion in the EEA Agreement of the EURES and PROGRESS axes of the Regulation. The Central Party has stated in the Committee that it wishes to oppose the government's submission to join part of the EURES axis of the EaSI programme and that it does not support the proposal for full support for the EaSI programme. At the same time, I would like to take the opportunity to thank the members of the Committee on Good and Effective Cooperation on a matter which concerns both Norway and Europe, and where it is known that the parties have different positions in several areas.
ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e4195	First, I want to thank the case spokesperson for a thorough work. For Norway, it is important to be able to continue to participate in European cooperation in the field of employment and social policy. Especially at a time of social unrest and high unemployment in Europe, it is important that Norway is a contributor. The Labour Party believes that the value of participating in such cooperation is great and considers it obvious that Norway should be involved in the process of creating a solid, inclusive and secure Europe. I believe that the EaSI programme is a positive contribution to this. The programme is a comprehensive three-axis EU programme which together helps to promote high employment, ensure decent social protection, combat social exclusion and poverty and improve working conditions. Until the end of 2013, Norway took part in two of these axes, PROGRESS and EURES. European cooperation was prioritised by the Stoltenberg Government. Under the PROGRESS programme, Norway had other national experts in the European Commission. The background for merging what was previously three independent applications, and creating a single application, was a desire to continue developing all areas of application, in particular EURES and PROGRESS, which are logically linked together as a whole. I think it is good for the government to continue to join the EURES axis, so that it can also help in the future to promote geographical mobility and increase employment opportunities by developing an open labour market. However, the brother part of the EaSI programme is not about it, but about the PROGRESS axis of the programme. In this context, the objective is to modernise labour and social policy. The main part of PROGRESS I think is in the middle of what we are working on in Norwegian labour and social policy, simply to promote employment – especially fighting youth unemployment – to promote social protection and inclusion, to fight against poverty and to improve conditions and conditions in the labour market. So by participating in PROGRESS, we support the development of appropriate social security and labour market policies by promoting good governance, mutual learning and social innovation. Therefore, when the government only partially endorses the new EaSI programme, it is a de-prioritisation of European cooperation in the field of employment and social affairs. The working party is critical of such a priority, especially because the foundation is budgetary. Making it an increase in employment and social solidarity in Europe should be a priority for Norway. In addition, participation in a larger part of the EaSI will allow Norway access to several tools, actions and programmes that can make it possible for more people with reduced working capacity and youth to enter the Norwegian labour market. I welcome this proposal where, on this basis, the Labour Party supports both the EURES and PROGRESS axes of the EaSI programme from the Norwegian side from 2015.

ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e4217	There are probably few themes that make the blood of otherwise wise nordics shower as much as any occasion for an EU or EEA debate. What helps Norwegian workers, Norwegian employers and Norwegian economy is, in my opinion, something we have to go in favour of. ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e4238	EURES is a cooperation network between the European Commission and the PES in the EU Member States, EEA countries and Switzerland. Norwegian participation in the EU Programme for Employment and Social Innovation, EaSI, for the period 2014-2020 is intended to contribute to the achievement of the Europe 2020 strategy. When I hear the representative Bjørdal talk about it contributing to a solidarity, inclusive and safe working life in Europe, I have to say it should flash some lamps for more than me. We see how little success reality looks in relation to the three honorary orders that were mentioned here. There are a lot of nice words, but nobody can avoid seeing what are the realities of the geographical area that we're talking about here. The centre party is opposed to today's agreement on the European Economic Area, EEA, which is based on the free flow of labour, capital, goods and services in this area, and thus the waiver of sovereignty. The representatives of the Central Party voted against the conclusion of the EEA Agreement by Norway in 1992 and, when we now experience the consequences in almost all areas of society – not least in the field of working life – more and more people also recognise here in Norway that voting against the EEA Agreement in 1992 was a wise position. The best way to increase employment in several European countries with a very high unemployment rate today will be for these countries to break out of the coercion line represented by the pan-European currency. The high unemployment rate and the high number of people moving between different European countries, combined with national characteristics, recently led the EU-critical parties to the election of the European Parliament in the year. The Central Party is confident that the time has come for more fundamental criticism of system failures in the European Union. The centre party points to Norway’s contribution to social cohesion through the EEA funds in several Eastern European countries, and it would be naive to believe that Norwegian accession to EURES will promote a higher level of employment overall in Europe. To that end, completely different instruments are required. I would also like to point out that since 2004, Norway has had a particularly large influx of labour from countries joining the European Union. There is therefore no problem for Norway as a whole to obtain the labour needed from these countries. Nor is it the extent of the large influx of labour that must be said to have become a problem for our nation, as Norwegian working life changes a lot by putting the pay and working conditions of different occupational groups under very great pressure. The Centre Party believes that Norway should strengthen efforts to combat the pressure on pay and working conditions in a number of professions, characterised by high levels of foreign labour. Norway must continue to provide financial support targeted at combating social exclusion and poverty in other European countries. The central party believes that the most important thing we can do as a maritime nation is to show other European countries that it is possible to maintain the Norwegian model with a high level of employment, for both men and women, and a good safety net through liberal, universal welfare schemes for our own inhabitants, seas in the economically demanding times. It demands that Norway work even more on what our nation can do about measures to increase the level of employment that has unfortunately been falling in recent years, thus weakening our model. One prerequisite for such a model is that we have our own monetary and financial policies. The Centre Party considers that EURES can be a fine and appropriate programme for several countries associated with the European Union, but does not consider it appropriate that Norway prioritises our funding to participate in this programme. The Centre Party will also indicate that it is voluntary for Norway to join this programme, and the Centre Party is therefore opposed to the Government’s submission to join part of the EURES axis of the EaSI programme. Nor will the centre party support the proposal for full support for the EaSI programme later on. That is why we are voting against the proposal for a decision.

ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e4309	I would like to start by thanking the Committee for a good and constructive cooperation. This proposition was referred to in Stortinget on Tuesday last week, and there has been great willingness from all the parties to deal with it before the summer, so that we can avoid delays in the development of the Harstad Road Package. The proposal contains not only the Road Package Harstad but also an additional element, namely a transitional arrangement for the interest compensation scheme for toll loans mentioned in Prop. 97 S for 2013–2014, which we discussed in Stortinget on Tuesday. It has made the treatment somewhat more demanding than whether it should be a pure toll proposition, as it also raises some fundamental questions. The Committee has considered Prop. 119 S in its entirety, including the transitional regime, but at the same time it is clear that a case has to be submitted concerning the interest compensation scheme for the Grand Duchy. So to the very contents of the road package Harstad. Work on the design of a new transport system in Harstad was undertaken due to the traffic challenges on the road. 83. The largest subproject is the construction of a 1,450-metre-long tunnel outside the centre to relieve the centre streets and future streets of the upper part of the city. In addition, various improvement measures shall be implemented along the lines of RV. 83, provision for more efficient and safe public transport, safer school roads, the establishment of car parking lots and bicycles at the end stops, and approx. 30 km with new pavements and walking and cycling routes. The package has a total cost estimate of 1.55 billion. SEK, of which 926 million. Tolls are tolls and the rest are State, municipal and county funds. Compared to local policy decisions, the State share has increased by 370 million. kr including VAT. The Government has submitted a proposal for toll collection based on eight toll stations with two-way collection in line with local policy decisions. At the same time, the Ministry is asked to obtain a mandate to assess whether it is more appropriate to add to one-way collection, thereby reducing the costs of collection. In addition, the Ministry would like to have a power to reduce the bomb charges based on a lower calculation technical interest rate under the transitional arrangements described in the Proposition. Both of these adaptations seem reasonable, and I hope they can be realised in cooperation with local authorities, in this case the municipality of Harstad and the municipality of Trom. The Ministry shows in Prop. 97 S for an interest-rate compensation scheme for toll loans. As a transitional arrangement and until a permanent arrangement is in place, for new toll projects that will be part of the new toll companies, it shall be possible to calculate tariffs based on a 10-year government bond rate. The transitional arrangement will apply to new projects for which the recovery has not been initiated by 20 June 2014. There are 13 such projects under consideration for the transitional regime. I expect that several people have already noticed that possibility. The purpose of derogating from the calculation technical interest rate of the State Road Service is for local authorities to assess for themselves whether they will use a lower interest rate in the calculation of the toll charges. Such an interest rate may not be lower than the 10-year government bond rate. If local authorities want to enter a risk premium in the interest rate, they will be up to the county and municipality, which also provides a guarantee. This mechanism raises some fundamental issues that the Committee has had relatively little time to consider. A Joint Committee therefore asks the Government to present a case on the interest-rate compensation scheme itself, which will form the basis for a broader discussion on the scheme. The transitional regime described in this proposition is, in the opinion of the undersigned, a good opportunity to adjust toll tariffs to a more realistic interest rate. It provides greater scope for action and more flexibility for local authorities, which today guarantee toll loans, to set lower tariffs. Local policy considerations are thus better addressed in such a scheme. I look forward to the further work on a toll reform and an interest-rate compensation scheme for toll loans. And then I am very happy that today we have adopted the Road Package Harstad, so that the work on the project can start without any delay.

Then we say that we have a transitional arrangement that takes us one step towards making sure that in the future the interest expense on that loan will be compensated. You get the loan together. Then you get a lower interest rate. It saves us money. Then, through the State budget, the state is going to compensate for the remaining interest expenditure. Thus, despite the lower fare, the drivers will have much less debt to pay down, or much less total cost, because now every penny they pay is going to pay down the loan itself instead of covering the interest expenses, because they're taking the state. Then you don't extend the discount, but you reduce the rates. The cost is reduced. Then you have to have a little bit bigger appropriations on the state budget, but you're going to save on total, because you don't need much interest, and you don't have any administration. Now I hope the representatives Eidsvoll Holmås and Pollestad have noted. It's kind of strange to hear red-green politicians who, every time this government does something smart, say it was in the NTP. I have to remind you that 6.5 pst. interest rate was also calculated on the basis of NTP. No change was adopted. It was not decided to have two or three toll companies. At best, you would have a toll company in each state. Then you don't get the same winnings. But I'm very happy that red-green politicians now think that what the government is proposing is so good that you'll be taking credit for it. Nothing is better than that we all stand together and defend the changes we propose. To the representative Juvik – the most expensive man in North Norway: I would like to remind you that this government has made sure that the toll costs for people in North Norway have dropped by at least 1.5 billion. kr. There's quite a lot of money, maybe not for a raiser for North Norway, but I think the business will notice it.
ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e4696	The representative Heikki Eidsvoll Holmås has spoken twice earlier in the debate and is given the floor to a brief remark, limited to 1 minute.
ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e4699	I just want to point out that I think it is problematic that the government comes to the Storting with syllable documents and non-slip conclusions that we have no opportunity to get into and be on, because no numbers have been presented. It is presented here as if this is very economical for drivers. Well, we actually don't know anything about that, because what we're invited to adopt here today is just something that will make it more expensive for drivers: the opportunity to extend the payoff time by putting down the tariffs.
ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e4704	I have no desire to draw the debate out in the late hours of the night, but, like the representative Heikki Eidsvoll Holmås, I must say that I am reacting very strongly to a Council of State which, at the end of the session, presents a matter of a very positive road package in Harstad, which they have waited a long time for, which contains several fundamental questions, and where a joint committee – I ask the Council of State to note the submission of both the case rapporteur and the committee leader – responds to the fact that this is presented to the principle assessment without the basis for which we can expect. When he then gets the word and the opportunity in the hall – through both line change and post – he does not use the time to inform the matter, but uses his usual tiara about the red-green ones. ParlaMint-NO_2014-06-19.ana.ud2279e4711	Let me draw your attention to the proposition, page 7, table 5.2. There is an overview of different borrowing rates, different tariffs, different financing costs and different collection costs. It says clearly that if you go from two-way collection to one-way collection, you will save 60 million. kr. That number has been documented. So here's a different point: if you go from 6.5 pst to 6.5 pst. to 2.8 pst. how the cost of financing goes down. Then you have to take into account that you also have to compensate for the interest expenses, that you have to collect the loan in order to bring down the interest rate. There's a real saving. Administrative protection is a real saving. But covering the remaining interest costs is the transfer of costs – there is no doubt about it. It goes from the drivers directly to the taxpayers. But already here it is seen that there are significant millions of dollars in real savings for the drivers in the Harstad area. If you don't want to look at the table, you can't do that. But now we've explained quite many times how the whole of this system is. I record that those who are now criticizing us and choosing to say that we are arrogant because we do not accept their arguments, relate to a small part of the whole and try to convince that that is only the case. But I think the majority in this room have seen where the whole thing is, that it's about administrative costs, real interest costs and the transfer of some of the costs from the drivers to the state budget. It is in line with the policy that this government wants. We're going to bring down the toll percentage, and that's exactly what we're delivering. At the same time, we're going to build multiple roads. That means we have to give more money to go, while at the same time we give money to delete some toll debts. That's what we get, because it's about prioritizing. It's about spending the money more efficiently. We have managed to speed up the design and planning of a dozen road projects. We have managed to increase the asphaltification in this country, even though the previous government has planned a reduction in asphaltification. We've got more railways in place. We've accelerated a lot of railway terminal work. It shows that what we are doing now, not at the expense of other transport projects, but that, in general, we give higher priority to transport. It's simply because we see this as a good investment. To reduce the cost of business is to secure jobs. Therefore, the entire employer’s fee scheme – as we did not get the results we wanted – will be compensated back to Nord-Norway. So it's not going to go away from the county. The money will come back again. In addition, we have decided to grant 1.5 billion. kr to compensate for interest payable by the red-green government on many toll projects — in Alta, now in Harstad, in Helgeland, and soon Bodø.
